39	
40	\subsection{Supporting Diagnostic Analyses}
41	
42	Four additional analyses confirm the mechanism underlying SICA's failure. Extraction error taxonomy (Appendix~\ref{app:extraction}): 91.8\% of constraints are technically discriminative, but their discriminative direction mirrors the majority answer; 42.9\% of constraints are redundant across traces (Appendix~\ref{app:constraint-quality}), quantifying how co-sourcing from the same model produces near-duplicate formalizations. Removing the 8.2\% non-discriminative constraints yields zero improvement. Constraint ablation (Appendix~\ref{app:ablation}): on Mistral-7B ($n = 804$, FOLIO + ProofWriter pooled), MAX-SAT and count-only agree on 94.4\% of predictions, with disagreements split nearly evenly (18 vs.\ 17); the solver's weighted satisfiability computation adds no value over simple constraint counting because constraint weights carry information redundant with vote counts (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). Per-domain, MAX-SAT provides a marginal edge in the intermediate-SC region (59.3\% vs.\ count-only 56.9\%) but actively hurts in the Type~C regime (36.5\% vs.\ 37.2\%), mirroring the regime-dependent pattern of SICA itself. Hyperparameter sensitivity (Appendix~\ref{app:sensitivity}): $K = 12$ is the only setting with $\Delta > 2$\,pp among five $K$ values, and only $T = 0.7$ among four temperatures yields a positive effect; both peaks coincide with relatively low SC baselines rather than reflecting hyperparameter effects per se. Entropy decomposition (Appendix~\ref{app:entropy}): all SICA effects concentrate in high-entropy problems ($\Delta$ up to $+12.8$\,pp in the intermediate-SC region), but the direction depends on the accuracy regime---high entropy signals correctable uncertainty only when the model has partial knowledge.
43	
44	\subsection{Remediation Analysis}
45	\label{sec:remediation}
46	\label{sec:interventions}
47	
48	We test twelve remediation strategies: nine prompt-level spanning six dimensions (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:remediation}) and three method-level (Table~\ref{tab:method_interventions}). All nine prompt-level strategies fail. Contrastive prompting reduces non-discriminative constraints from 8.2\% to 6.0\% but newly discriminative constraints still mirror the majority ($\Delta = -1.1$\,pp). Cross-model extraction fails in both directions: weaker models extracting from stronger traces yield 0\% discriminative constraints; stronger models extracting from weaker traces produce accuracy within $\pm$1\,pp of self-extraction. Only 1 of 4 temperature settings yields a positive effect ($p = 0.092$); multi-temperature sampling produces $\Delta = 0.00$\,pp.
49	
50	\paragraph{Method-level interventions.} Three method-level strategies target different aspects of the independence bottleneck on Mistral-7B (Table~\ref{tab:method_interventions}). Independent Constraint Generation (ICG) bypasses trace-constraint coupling by generating constraints directly from premises. Despite producing objectively superior constraints---higher Z3 compilation, lower redundancy (Appendix~\ref{app:constraint-quality})---ICG yields $\Delta \approx 0$ across three seeds (McNemar $p = .206$, n.s.). Per-constraint-set reranking produces $\kappa = 0.47$, nearly identical to SC's $\kappa = 0.44$: the bottleneck is a property of the model itself, not an artifact of trace co-sourcing. Contrastive scoring generates pro and con constraints per candidate but yields $\Delta = -9.31$\,pp on FOLIO ($p < 0.01$) and $-4.50$\,pp on LogiQA (n.s.); 62.7\% of constraints are trivially satisfied, explaining systematic degeneration (Appendices~\ref{app:constraint-quality},~\ref{app:error-categorization}). Debate adds a proposer-critic-defense chain, producing $\Delta = -18.63$\,pp and $-23.00$\,pp ($p < 0.001$ on both datasets), with discordant pairs heavily skewed toward correct$\to$wrong flips (Appendix~\ref{app:error-categorization}). Three of six method-dataset conditions reach significance, all negative.
51	
52	\begin{table}[t]
53	\centering
54	\small
55	\caption{Method-level interventions on Mistral-7B with McNemar's paired test. All produce neutral-to-catastrophic results. ICG shows two FOLIO seeds; $p$ for the first. {*}{*}{*}\,$p < 0.001$; {*}{*}\,$p < 0.01$.}
56	\label{tab:method_interventions}
57	\begin{tabular}{@{}ll rc rc@{}}
58	\toprule
59	& & \multicolumn{2}{c}{FOLIO ($n = 204$)} & \multicolumn{2}{c}{LogiQA ($n = 200$)} \\
60	\cmidrule(lr){3-4} \cmidrule(lr){5-6}
61	Method & Target & $\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ & $\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\
62	\midrule
63	SC baseline & --- & 0.00 & --- & 0.00 & --- \\
64	ICG & Trace coupling & $-$1.96\,/\,$+$1.47 & .206 & $-$0.50 & .706 \\
65	Contrastive & Scoring bias & $\mathbf{-9.31}$ & .007 & $-$4.50 & .160 \\
66	Debate & Role diversity & $\mathbf{-18.63}$ & ${<}.001$ & $\mathbf{-23.00}$ & ${<}.001$ \\
67	\bottomrule
68	\end{tabular}
69	\end{table}
70	
71	\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} compares Fleiss' $\kappa$ across four aggregation methods on Mistral-7B FOLIO and adds SC from three model families for cross-architecture validation. On Mistral-7B, SC ($\kappa = 0.44$), Diverse-Prompt ($\kappa = 0.40$), and ICG ($\kappa = 0.47$) cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling. Debate breaks through to $\kappa = 0.15$ (Eff-$K = 4.44$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp. All three model families cluster above $\kappa = 0.40$, confirming the ceiling is architecture-independent (Appendix~\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}). This constitutes a Pareto frontier (Figure~\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}): methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy cannot break the correlation floor. The only demonstrated path past this frontier requires signal from outside the model's distribution, consistent with the oracle result (\S\ref{sec:oracle}) and published systems that bypass self-extraction \citep{xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, pan2023logiclm}.
72

```text
1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	\subsection{Setup}
5	\label{sec:exp-setup}
6	
7	We evaluate SICA against Self-Consistency (SC) across six models on three reasoning domains with over 3,000 instances.
8	
9	\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B parameters).
10	
11	\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, with gold FOL annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$). StrategyQA~\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$) serves as a domain-formalizability diagnostic. GSM8K~\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\%$).
12	
13	\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$; extraction at $T_{\text{ext}}{=}0.3$. Z3 timeout: 10s. $\alpha{=}0.5$. Statistical significance via McNemar exact test ($\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\% bootstrap CIs from 10,000 resamples for $n \geq 200$.
14	
15	
16	\subsection{Main Results}
17	\label{sec:main-results}
18	
19	Table~\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all conditions sorted by SC baseline. SICA effectiveness depends sharply on the accuracy regime.
20	
21	\begin{table*}[t]
22	\centering
23	\small
24	\caption{SICA vs.\ SC across all conditions sorted by SC accuracy. $\Delta$ = SICA $-$ SC (pp). $^b$In-context learning with gold FOL examples. $\dagger$\,$n < 100$ (suggestive only). \textbf{Bold $\Delta$}: $p < 0.05$ (McNemar). {*}\,$p < 0.05$;\enspace{**}\,$p < 0.01$;\enspace{***}\,$p < 0.001$. Failure modes detailed in Appendix~\ref{app:failure-modes}.}
25	\label{tab:main_results}
26	\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrl@{}}
27	\toprule
28	Model & Domain & $n$ & SC (\%) & SICA (\%) & $\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\
29	\midrule
30	\multicolumn{7}{l}{\textit{Type C: error amplification (SC $\leq$ 40\%)}} \\
31	Mistral-7B & PW-D5 & 600 & 39.33 & 36.50 & $\mathbf{-2.83}$ & 0.014{*} \\
32	\midrule
33	\multicolumn{7}{l}{\textit{Intermediate SC (SC $\sim$50--55\%, Mistral-7B only)}} \\
34	Mistral-7B & LogiQA & 200 & 51.00 & 51.50 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\
35	Mistral-7B & FOLIO (s123) & 204 & 54.41 & 57.35 & $+$2.94 & 0.070 \\
36	Mistral-7B & FOLIO (s42) & 204 & 55.88 & \textbf{59.31} & $+$3.43 & 0.092 \\
37	Mistral-7B & FOLIO (s456) & 204 & 55.88 & 55.88 & 0.00 & 1.000 \\
38	Mistral-7B & FOLIO (s789) & 204 & 54.41 & 53.92 & $-$0.49 & 1.000 \\
39	\midrule
40	\multicolumn{7}{l}{\textit{Type A: confirmation bias (SC $\geq$ 60\%)}} \\
41	LLaMA-3.1-8B & FOLIO & 204 & 66.18 & 61.76 & $-$4.41 & 0.093 \\
42	LLaMA-3.1-8B & StrategyQA & 200 & 72.00 & 74.00 & $+$2.00 & 0.424 \\
43	Qwen2.5-14B & FOLIO (ICL)$^b$ & 199 & 74.37 & 74.87 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\
44	Qwen2.5-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 75.17 & 72.67 & $\mathbf{-2.50}$ & 0.011{*} \\
45	Qwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.49 & 74.51 & $-$0.98 & 1.000 \\
46	Qwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.98 & 76.47 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\
47	Qwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 200 & 78.50 & 78.00 & $-$0.50 & 1.000 \\
48	Qwen3-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 84.80 & \textbf{84.31} & $-$0.49 & 1.000 \\
49	Qwen3-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 85.00 & 85.67 & $+$0.67 & 1.000 \\
50	\midrule
51	\multicolumn{7}{l}{\textit{Degenerate ($C_0 < 5\%$)}} \\
52	QwQ-32B & AIME 2024$^\dagger$ & 22 & 72.70 & 72.70 & 0.00 & \textrm{---} \\
53	Qwen2.5-14B & GSM8K$^\dagger$ & 30 & 96.67 & 96.67 & 0.00 & \textrm{---} \\
54	\midrule
55	\multicolumn{7}{l}{\textit{Oracle upper bound (gold FOL/DSL + Z3)}} \\
56	Qwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.49 & \textbf{89.71} & $\mathbf{+14.22}$ & $2.0{\times}10^{-7}${***} \\
57	Qwen2.5-14B & ProofWriter & 600 & 75.17 & \textbf{86.67} & $\mathbf{+11.50}$ & $9.21{\times}10^{-15}${***} \\
58	\bottomrule
59	\end{tabular}
60	\end{table*}
61	
62	\paragraph{Intermediate-SC region.} Mistral-7B on FOLIO (SC $\sim$50--55\%) shows positive directional trends in 2 of 4 seeds: $\Delta = +3.43$\,pp ($p = 0.092$) and $+2.94$\,pp ($p = 0.070$); the remaining seeds show null or negative results. No seed reaches $p < 0.05$; the 4-seed mean is $+1.47$\,pp. Mistral-7B on LogiQA, despite the same SC range, yields only $\Delta = +0.50$\,pp ($p = 1.0$), attributable to domain formalizability (Appendix~\ref{app:failure-modes}).
63	
64	\paragraph{Type A (SC $\geq$ 60\%): confirmation bias.} Nine conditions with SC $\geq 63\%$ (combined $N > 2{,}800$) produce no significant positive effects. Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter shows significant degradation ($\Delta = -2.50$\,pp, $p = 0.011$). Even in-context learning with gold FOL examples ($\Delta = +0.50$\,pp, n.s.) cannot break confirmation bias.
65	
66	\paragraph{Type C (SC $\leq$ 40\%): error amplification.} Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($n = 600$, SC $= 39.33\%$) shows $\Delta = -2.83$\,pp ($p = 0.014$): SICA \emph{significantly hurts} by formalizing incorrect reasoning.
67	
68	\paragraph{Degenerate cases.} When $C_0 < 5\%$, nearly all traces agree and SICA reduces to SC by construction.
69	
70	Figure~\ref{fig:regime-scatter} visualizes the regime dependence: the intermediate-SC region is narrow and observed only for Mistral-7B on FOLIO.
71	
72	\begin{figure}[t]
73	\centering
74	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{f2_regime_scatter}
75	\caption{SICA $\Delta$ (pp) vs.\ SC baseline. Filled: $n \geq 100$; open: $n < 100$. The intermediate-SC region (50--55\%, shaded) contains the only positive trends (none reaching $p < 0.05$). Type~C: significant degradation ($p = 0.014$).}
76	\label{fig:regime-scatter}
77	\end{figure}
78	
79	
80	\subsection{Oracle Control}
81	\label{sec:oracle}
82	
83	Oracle variants replace self-extracted constraints with gold annotations: FOL from FOLIO ($n = 204$) and DSL from ProofWriter ($n = 600$). Oracle-SICA achieves 89.71\% on FOLIO ($\Delta = +14.22$\,pp, $p = 2.0 \times 10^{-7}$) and 86.67\% on ProofWriter ($\Delta = +11.50$\,pp, $p = 9.21 \times 10^{-15}$). Z3 agrees with gold labels on 95.1\% (FOLIO) and 97.1\% (ProofWriter) of problems.
84	
85	These results establish that the MAX-SAT mechanism is sound given discriminative constraints. Mistral-7B's best self-extracted result ($+3.43$\,pp) captures only 24.1\% of the FOLIO oracle ceiling. The remaining 75.9\% is lost to self-extraction quality.
86	
87	
88	\subsection{Four-Seed Replication}
89	\label{sec:replication}
90	
91	We replicate the intermediate-SC result with four seeds on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$, $K = 12$, $T = 0.7$). Effects range from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\,pp (mean $+1.47$\,pp); 2 of 4 seeds show marginal trends ($p < 0.10$), none reaches $p < 0.05$. Seeds~42 and~456 share identical SC baselines (55.88\%) yet produce different outcomes ($+3.43$ vs.\ $0.00$\,pp), indicating that seed-level constraint quality---not SC level alone---drives the effect. The entropy decomposition in Appendix~\ref{app:entropy} confirms that the aggregate $\Delta$ is driven by high-entropy problems: seed~42 achieves $+12.8$\,pp on this partition while seed~456 shows $-1.3$\,pp.
```

7	## Part 1: Refined Section Outline

9	### §1 Introduction (1.35pp)
10	
11	**¶1 — Hook: SC plateau**
12	- Test-time compute via SC improves reasoning but plateaus (Loo 2025)
13	- Adding more traces yields diminishing returns → structural mismatch
14	- Key number: gains plateau and even decline for strong models
15	
16	**¶2 — Gap: Can self-extraction break the plateau?**
17	- Neuro-symbolic methods (Logic-LM, DyLoS, FoVer, Aristotle) use external/trained components
18	- Simpler question: can the SAME model extract useful FOL constraints from its own traces?
19	- Self-extraction = zero-cost upgrade, no external tools needed
20	
21	**¶3 — Insight: CJT + Confirmation Bias** [MAJOR UPDATE]
22	- CJT requires voter independence; same-model traces violate this
23	- Eff-K = 1.20–3.62 for nominal K=12 → traces beyond 12 add negligible info
24	- [NEW] Self-extracted constraints don't just inherit correlation — they exhibit Symbolic Confirmation Bias (4.97× bias ratio on incorrect problems)
25	- [NEW] Process-level analysis: CoT cosine similarity 0.69 >> answer-κ 0.41 → reasoning paths more homogeneous than answers suggest
26	
27	**¶4 — Evaluation scope**
28	- SICA pipeline + Condorcet analysis + oracle control
29	- 6 models (7B–32B), 3 domains, N>3,000, 4-seed replication
30	- [UPDATE] 25+ remediation strategies (up from 12)
31	
32	**¶5 — Key results**
33	- No significant positive; oracle +14.22pp proves mechanism sound
34	- Independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate κ→0.15 but -18.6pp
35	- Bottleneck universal across 3 model families (Mistral, LLaMA, Qwen)
